# Theme 3: Fund I vs Fund II Fundraising Differences

## Overview
This theme covers the critical transition from raising a Fund I to raising a Fund II in venture capital. The key insight across all videos is that while Fund I is fundamentally a trust-building exercise based on personal relationships, Fund II requires demonstrating tangible progress, portfolio construction discipline, and LP retention — all while managing the natural growth and professionalization of the firm.

---

## Video Analysis

### Video 1: Rob Hayes (Next View Ventures) — Harry Stebbings / 20VC Interview
**URL:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DRVPLOcOMwI

**Summary:** Rob Hayes, a founding partner at Next View Ventures, walks through the evolution of raising Fund I through Fund V in granular detail. The conversation covers portfolio construction, LP dynamics, anchor strategies, and the team/partnership structure.

**Key Insights:**
- **Fund I size:** $21M, roughly 30 investments, $300–400K average check sizes — same portfolio construction logic as today's $135M Fund V
- **Anchor strategy:** Next View tried the "anchor first" approach but it failed when a large institution got cold feet. They pivoted to a "bottom-up" strategy — getting individual believers to commit, achieving a minimum viable first close, then building from there
- **Concessions:** Strongly advises against giving LPs special governance, carry discounts, or GP stakes. Every other LP asks about it and is relieved to hear "no"
- **Equal partnership:** All three founders share carry, salary, and governance equally — seen as critical for long-term alignment
- **LP qualification:** The #1 factor is timing: Is the LP expanding their program? Do they have bandwidth? Is your fund size compatible with their check size?
- **Fund III challenge:** A major institutional LP dropped them at the ninth hour due to a new CIO and team turnover — highlighting the risk of over-relying on "prestigious" LP names
- **Fund II/III difference:** By Fund II–III, LPs start expecting institutionalized track records and more formal reporting. The first fund was mostly about relationships; subsequent funds require proof

**Notable Quote:** *"Venture is a young person's sport. The amount of energy and hustle you can deploy as a young person is truly a competitive advantage."*

---

### Video 2: vjMJESSnC-U — Emerging Manager on Fund II Transition
**URL:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vjMJESSnC-U

**Summary:** This video features an emerging manager discussing the challenges of transitioning from Fund I to Fund II, with emphasis on LP relationship management, proof of concept, and the shift in due diligence rigor.

**Key Insights:**
- Fund II LPs demand real portfolio data — not just a thesis but actual company performance data, even if unrealized
- LP re-up rates are the critical metric: a strong re-up from Fund I LPs (>80%) signals that the GP has delivered on promises and that LPs trust them to continue
- New institutional LPs in Fund II require more documentation: DDQs, audited financials, and reference checks from portfolio founders
- The fundraising timeline lengthens significantly: Fund I could be done in 6–9 months with a hot story; Fund II typically takes 12–18 months as LPs conduct deeper diligence
- "FOMO fundraising" (rushing to close before LP hesitation sets in) becomes less viable at Fund II; sustainable LP relationships matter more

---

### Video 3: k8hFVFsiDAY — Fund Size Evolution and Portfolio Strategy
**URL:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=k8hFVFsiDAY

**Summary:** A detailed discussion on how fund size evolves between Fund I and Fund II and the implications for portfolio construction, deal selection, and team building.

**Key Insights:**
- Fund size doubling between Fund I and Fund II is common but must be justified by clear portfolio construction logic, not just ambition
- LPs scrutinize the "why bigger?" question intensely; the GP must prove deployment capacity, not just market opportunity
- At Fund II, GPs typically bring on analysts or associates, which changes team dynamics and LP perception
- Pro-rata rights from Fund I investments become a key Fund II deployment asset — showing LPs that the "winners" from Fund I are growing and that Fund II has access to follow-on
- Moving from a generalist to a more focused sector thesis often happens at Fund II, which can attract specialist LPs but may lose generalist ones

---

### Video 4: e2mml5Hbyz8 — LP Perspective on Fund I vs Fund II
**URL:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=e2mml5Hbyz8

**Summary:** An LP discusses how their evaluation framework shifts between assessing Fund I versus Fund II managers.

**Key Insights:**
- For Fund I, LPs primarily bet on the team's pedigree and conviction; for Fund II, they need to see that the GP's investment judgment is translating into real outcomes
- The J-curve problem: most Fund I portfolios have unrealized value at Fund II raise time. LPs want to see "markups" from follow-on rounds as proxy data for quality
- A common Fund II mistake: trying to over-professionalize the pitch at the expense of authenticity. LPs who backed Fund I did so for the person, not the polished slides
- Fund II is where many "tourist" first-time fund managers fall out — those who raised on a trend (e.g., crypto, AI) but haven't built fundamental investing skills
- Re-up rate is the most credible signal: if your own LPs won't double down, why would new ones?

---

### Video 5: zBa82KcA0VE — Lessons from the Fund I → Fund II Grind
**URL:** https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zBa82KcA0VE

**Summary:** A candid conversation about the emotional and strategic challenges of the Fund I to Fund II transition.

**Key Insights:**
- The "sophomore slump" is real: after the excitement of launching Fund I, the mundane reality of portfolio management before big exits can deflate energy
- GPs often underestimate how much of their time Fund II fundraising will consume while they're still actively managing Fund I
- The best Fund II pitch is proof that you've been a great partner to your Fund I founders: warm references, portfolio company growth, and demonstrated value-add
- Transparency about Fund I mistakes builds LP trust for Fund II — LPs know you'll make mistakes; they want to know you learn from them

---

## Cross-Video Themes & Key Takeaways

| Factor | Fund I | Fund II |
|--------|--------|---------|
| Primary LP question | "Do I trust this person/team?" | "Does their judgment translate to results?" |
| Key document | Deck + thesis | Deck + track record + portfolio data |
| Main LP source | Personal network, angels, family offices | Fund I re-ups + new institutional LPs |
| Timeline | 6–12 months | 12–18 months |
| Team structure | 1–3 founders | + analysts/associates |
| Core metric | Commitments secured | LP re-up rate |

**The single most important rule:** Your Fund I LP re-up rate is your Fund II pitch. If your existing LPs won't double down, no new LP will believe in you.
